# Project progress report

This is the live, append-only progress record requested by the user on 11 October
2026. Earlier milestones are indexed in
[the V2 development trail](PROJECT_DEVELOPMENT_AUDIT_TRAIL_V2.md).
Sealed study reports retain their original contents. Each new completed study
will include a dated snapshot of this live report in its artifact index.

## 2026-10-12 00:57 UTC — Matched T5 observation comparison started

**Authorization:** continue the proposed narrower comparison and always record
progress in the report. No fitting or new population simulation is authorized
by this bounded study.

**Starting evidence:** the image-clamp repair removed the causal baseline error;
all 256 extended grating trajectories converged. Horizontal mean criteria passed
16/32 conditions, despite correct raw direction in all conditions. See
[the repair report](PHYSIOLOGICAL_IMAGE_CLAMP_REPAIR_REPORT_V1.md).
This does not establish biological validation or explain texture sign reversals.

**Current action:** audit the published T5 cell-7/iteration-52 parameters and
training/selection provenance, then freeze observation-scoring rules before
calculating new grating scores. The existing measured archive contains eight
native grating conditions: two dwell times, two starting phases and two opposing
directions. Preserve all eight. Their native periods are 320 and 1,280 ms;
the 320-ms condition differs from our earlier 500-ms image stimulus.

**Reporting change:** added the persistent reporting rule in
AGENTS.md (not bundled: `AGENTS.md`). The live files will remain outside sealed indexes;
per-study snapshots will preserve the report at completion. No new observation
scores have yet been calculated. The current Git checkpoint is
`eefdc08dd3fea30a89098afcdcee704ea8f8cdcc`.


## 2026-10-12T01:04:18.025525+00:00 — T5 training provenance verified

All 44 fitting traces/clocks are bitwise subsets of the full single-bar data. Only 24 width-two trials (3,748 samples) enter the audited objective; no grating samples enter it. Archived objective 1.6034450300285 mV² reproduces the saved value to floating-point precision. The fixed example comes from the published plotting script. Its author selection history remains unknown; this is stimulus-held-out relative to that objective, not independent-cell or fully blind validation. No new grating scores inspected. [Evidence](../results/t5_matched_grating_observation_v1/provenance.json).

## 2026-10-12T01:04:45.095002+00:00 — Matched observation methods frozen

The protocol, input hashes, unchanged parameters, analysis/review code and tests are frozen before new grating scores. Both primary readouts use actual recorded clocks and cycles 2–3; all eight observations and four direction pairs are included. The engineering thresholds are not physiological confidence intervals. Four predecessor indexes and all 10,048 indexed entries passed verification (entries overlap). [Protocol](T5_MATCHED_GRATING_OBSERVATION_PROTOCOL_V1.md).

## 2026-10-12T01:04:58.159103+00:00 — All eight native grating observations scored

Matched finite-window scoring completed for 9,248 recorded samples and four direction pairs. Expected-direction engineering passes: observed mean 3/4, observed amplitude 4/4; model mean 0/4, model amplitude 4/4. All responses and failures retained; independent technical review remains pending. No population or biological validity claim follows from these counts. [Complete scores](../results/t5_matched_grating_observation_v1/analysis.json).

## 2026-10-12T01:04:58.414174+00:00 — Matched comparison passed separate numerical and arithmetic review

All eight raw recording extractions/clocks and the parameter vector match bitwise. The saved independent author-RHS solver agrees within 2.11e-10 mV; independent sums/quantiles/harmonic arithmetic differ by at most 1.07e-14. All 48 primary/secondary pair scores and failure flags were checked separately. This validates alignment and computation, not independent biological generalization. [Review](../results/t5_matched_grating_observation_v1/independent_review.json).

## 2026-10-12T01:08:38.920043+00:00 — Matched observation report and figures completed

The native fixed T5 model preserves amplitude direction in all four pairs but understates mean direction strength in the three pairs with expected recorded means. The fourth recorded mean is opposite to amplitude preference and remains in the result. Slow model DSIs (~0.0915) are close to the project cutoff, so report magnitudes rather than only 0/4 passes. Waveform RMSE over scored samples is 4.020545 mV. No parameter refit, image-transfer validation or degeneracy conclusion. [Full report](T5_MATCHED_GRATING_OBSERVATION_REPORT_V1.md), [simple summary](T5_MATCHED_GRATING_OBSERVATION_TLDR_V1.md). Next proposed task: fixed-parameter image-to-native amplitude comparison with a declared spatial mapping; it has not been run. Final preservation, Git checkpoint and artifact seal remain pending.

## 2026-10-12T01:51:51.557486+00:00 — Image-to-native T5 transfer study started

The user authorized the proposed next comparison. Keep published cell-7/iteration-52 parameters and all eight native grating conditions. Test the eight previously selected T5 geometry templates, which are computational placements of the same physiological model, not eight new recorded neurons. Audit the actual production image sampler with the native dark/background, discrete phase sequence and nine-column longitudinal aperture. Primary raster/frame resolution is the existing population 1°/5-ms setting; prospectively compare 0.5° and 0.25° pixels and 2.5-ms frames. No new simulation, fit, model implementation or positive-result claim in this assessment.

Completed all 1,500 reset episodes: 600 simulated seconds, 60,000 paired updates and 121,500 raw frame timings in 538.83 wall seconds. ## 2026-10-24 — Sampling geometry and readout sensitivity explained during fixed execution

A supplemental code-level audit checks all eight eye/panorama-pitch combinations on the unchanged integer-angle receptor grid. Bilinear weights are exactly 0.5 on each axis, so each receptor value is a quarter-weight average of four corner point samples at azimuth/elevation coordinate offsets ±pitch/2, followed by float32 rounding. Refining the panorama therefore shrinks an implicit interpolation footprint toward a direct point sample. This explains a change in input smoothing; it does not establish measured photoreceptor optics or identify the sole source of neural errors. The geometric result is independent of favorable neural responses. [Exact sampler check](../results/image_sampling_diagnostic_v1/sampler_footprint_v1.json).

A separate post-protocol explanatory calculation records the twelve original readouts' feature coefficients after standardization and the bound |delta score| ≤ (|bL/scaleL|+|bR/scaleR|) times the largest left/right feature error. A bound smaller than the reference score's distance to either cutoff is sufficient for categorical stability. This is an explanation of sensitivity, not a new gate, fit or alteration of the frozen criteria. [Coefficients/bounds](../results/image_sampling_diagnostic_v1/decoder_explanation_v1/fixed_decoder_sensitivity.csv), [scope](../results/image_sampling_diagnostic_v1/decoder_explanation_v1/provenance.json).

## 2026-10-26 — Independent feedback reviewed against saved evidence

Reviewed the user's external second opinion against the code, sealed study and a selection of primary publications. Image-derived structure-tensor confidence weighting is a reasonable next engineering hypothesis: the existing image estimator uses confidence and geometric weights, while the registered spatial readout uses geometric weights alone. They also differ in spatial support, channel interpolation and clipping, so the existing comparison does not establish which difference caused the performance gap. The confidence would be an additional image-derived signal, not a demonstrated biological neural mechanism. Previously inspected z rooms cannot serve as an independent test of a method developed using them; use a newly generated held-out set and retain the existing frozen success criteria. The feedback's 44/48 joint-detection and RMS requirements are proposed new criteria, not the historical acceptance rules.

Corrections to the external review: the larger structural export omits CT1; the recovered 230,681 CT1 synapses do not contain the local synapse/branch positions or inhibitory dynamics needed to reconstruct local CT1 normalization. The frozen 13,593-node circuit does not contain VS or uLPTCrn; adding them requires a separately evidenced graph. Its comparison of 0.5° and 1° error rates uses different room sets, so it cannot isolate a sampling effect. Claimed aliasing, confidence benefits and unique causes require controlled tests. The correct Pokusaeva 2024 article identifier is [10.1038/s41467-024-53173-w](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-53173-w). The distinction between observed rotation and unintended rotation is material: the current open-loop trials prescribe turn labels and do not infer the animal's intention. Nonvisual locomotor signals in HS cells are supported by [Fujiwara et al. 2017](https://www.nature.com/articles/nn.4435), but a command-comparison mechanism and its parameters have not been established here. No simulation, new detector, physiological validation, changed acceptance rule or closed-loop controller was produced in this review.

## 2026-10-27 — Alternatives required at each research decision

The user requests explicit alternatives at every step, including what each can establish, its limitations and a recommended next action. Going forward, each proposed experiment should state the decision its outcomes would support before execution, then present the remaining alternatives after reporting results. This communication requirement changes no sealed result or scientific acceptance rule.

Based on the [completed failure-localization study](BIOLOGICAL_FAILURE_LOCALIZATION_REPORT_V1.md), the immediate options are: separate uLPTCrn-family and H2rn-family silencing in the recording-stimulus benchmark where joint activity improved bIPS; investigate the remaining DNp15 stimulus/compartment/measurement mismatch using evidence-supported mappings and independent recordings if available; or test a justified, frozen candidate on new small-yaw environments. Earlier anatomical walking analyses already tested individual-family lesions and did not establish general small-yaw benefit, so the first option is a new stimulus-specific mechanism comparison, not a claim that those lesions have never been tested. Recommend the focused mechanism test together with the measurement/provenance audit, followed by new small-turn testing only if a defensible candidate emerges. No new experiment, biological validation, reconstruction or parameter selection was performed in this decision update.

## 2026-10-31 — Primary neural-model objective reaffirmed

The user clarifies that the central objective remains a biologically grounded neural model with reliable detection from neural activity alone. ## 2026-10-31 — Existing published neural approaches clarified

Checked primary literature for existing neural models relevant to the user's goal. Erginkaya et al. (2025), https://www.nature.com/articles/s41593-025-01948-9, already cited in the original assignment, includes a simplified recurrent/disinhibitory HS/H2–bIPS/uLPTCrn–DNp15 model calibrated to neural responses and used to study rotation extraction during forward movement. Lappalainen et al. (2024), https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-024-07939-3, provides the upstream connectome-constrained, task-optimized FlyVis model; it is not itself a complete MaleCNS HS/bIPS/DNp15 yaw model. The earlier [frontend report](PROFESSOR_FUNCTIONAL_FRONTEND_TECHNICAL_REPORT_V1.md) already records unsuccessful frozen FlyVis integration checks, so FlyVis is not a newly discovered or automatically interchangeable solution. These publications support using established mechanisms as references, but do not establish the exact +/-2-degree/s, speed-specific false-alarm criteria for our implementation. No new reconstruction, simulation or physiological validation was performed; all frozen models/results remain unchanged.

The full round therefore has 16 fitted readers and 44 fixed reader/policy combinations, with no further repair bank planned. [All temporal outcomes](../results/small_turn_round_v1/analysis/temporal_summary.json). Reduced-model fitting/scoring and independent review remain to be completed; no fresh test image has been rendered.

All reduced fits and calibration outcomes completed with no qualifying model at 190, 490 or 990 ms. The best worst-speed new readout is spatial MaleCNS nuisance ridge: 20/32, 15/32 and 14/32 small-turn detections at slow/medium/fast speeds, with straight alarms 0/16, 0/16 and 1/16 under its budgeted policy. The ratio reader gives 32/32, 17/32 and 9/32 with the same alarm counts. Three base MaleCNS readers get raw direction correct for every actual small turn but fail reliable detection because some straight-walking peaks overlap turn scores. Fixed-score negative-budget certificates cannot establish a universal limit of neural information or a single biological cause. The source-reduced circuit's horizontal transfer remains exploratory; it has not been fitted or validated against new recordings.

All 108 aperture/time/solver controls finish. Independent review reproduces 199,752 base and 77,952 temporal predictions within 1.09e-14/9.33e-15, all 264 policy summaries, all 1,944 numerical-policy rows, all 640 reduced input-artifact receipts and all 976 frozen prior inputs. A fresh whole walking replay through literal author functions reproduces the image hash and both copies' feature vectors exactly after declared storage. Nine focused tests pass. [Curves](../results/small_turn_round_v1/analysis/full_curve_review.json), [decisions](../results/small_turn_round_v1/analysis/decision_review.json), [literal replay](../results/small_turn_round_v1/analysis/literal_full_path_replay.json), [numerics/preservation](../results/small_turn_round_v1/analysis/numerical_independent_review.json). This is separate from the preserved scalar-export correction and does not alter scientific outcomes.

A genuinely different alternative would train a separately versioned neural visual encoder on diverse walking optic flow and benchmark physiology independently; no such encoder training or new biological benchmark occurred in this round. ## 2026-11-06 — Published-model comparison and bounded next milestone advised

The user asked whether further biological validation is justified and whether comparable models succeed. A primary-source literature check finds concrete, restricted precedents: [Gruntman2018](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5967973/) predicts moving-stimulus T4 voltage using a passive conductance model; [Lappalainen2024](https://doi.org/10.1038/s41586-024-07939-3) trains connectome-constrained visual networks on optic-flow estimation and evaluates physiology separately, including correct ensemble contrast preferences for32 characterized cell types and T4/T5 motion tuning. These results do not establish our MaleCNS integration, downstream waveform accuracy or2deg/s walking-yaw performance. [Official FlyVis documentation](https://turagalab.github.io/flyvis/install/) provides pretrained models, making a bounded external-model benchmark an alternative to training a new encoder. No new install, model download, training, neural solve or validation claim accompanies this advisory check.

Recommendation: retain a biologically supported yaw model as the goal, and define the next finite milestone as a source-native published T4 reference reproduction followed by a prospectively specified comparison on suitable independent responses. Biologically constrained calibration is permitted in a separately versioned candidate with development/test separation; the original baseline stays sealed. Benchmarking pretrained FlyVis is a parallel scientific alternative to consider if the smaller reference route fails, with explicit input/output and connectome-provenance checks before integration. Repeated scoring of unchanged candidates on inspected recordings would not provide new independent validation. This is advice based on literature and the completed negative assay, not a newly executed comparative study or evidence that either alternative will necessarily succeed.

## 2026-11-13 — Targeted comparison with published fly-vision studies

At the user's request, compared the current handoff and T4 reserved-transfer report with selected primary studies on connectome-constrained optic-flow modeling, T4 voltage-to-output transformation, T5 contrast processing, HS–H2 electrical coupling, behavioral-state modulation and the bIPS circuit. The comparison reinforces the existing integration priority: a T4 somatic-voltage prediction does not identify axonal calcium or synaptic release, and a voltage-to-network drive remains an explicit, separately testable assumption. [T4 voltage-to-calcium study](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10082464/); [FlyVis study](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-024-07939-3); [contrast gain study](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-52724-5); [HS–H2 electrical study](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-53173-w); [state modulation study](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC5776785/); [bIPS study](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12148931/).

The review also identifies evidence-backed checks for ON/OFF coverage, retinal geometry and temporal sampling, contrast/luminance dependence, and downstream motion-opponent responses. These are known project gaps or partial capabilities, not newly demonstrated defects; LPi circuitry and functional T5 channels already exist in separate project versions, but have not received full integrated physiological qualification. Electrical coupling and behavioral state warrant explicit limitations and later sensitivity work, not invented parameters or automatic additions. Existing T4c population voltage/fluorescence transfer cannot identify horizontal-cell synaptic release. This is a targeted literature comparison, not an exhaustive search, a new numerical experiment, or biological validation. No sealed evidence or V9 files changed.

## 2026-11-13 — External deep-research feedback reviewed

Reviewed the user-supplied literature report against project history and selected primary publications. [Mishra et al.](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10082464/) support a nonlinear T4c indicator-voltage-to-calcium relation, not a calibrated horizontal T4 mV-to-release map. The recent [Vieira et al. study](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13643278/) measures channel localization/gating and models active T5 responses; its T4 channel observations and illustrative simulation do not establish an active T4a/b release mapping. Existing walking and physiology outcomes used in development are inspected, and reserved status must be checked per dataset before any new holdout claim. A bridge-sensitivity failure would show sensitivity under the tested assumptions, not by itself prove biological non-identifiability. No simulations, downloads, model edits, sealed-artifact changes or additional biological validation were performed.

The [prospective protocol](H2_T4_RECORDING_COMPARISON_PROTOCOL_V1.md) specifies all left-eye T4 sources through the published cable, functional T5, unchanged LPi graph, a fixed model-unit bridge, the identical saved approximate H2 stimulus reconstructions, model-versus-recorded directional error and an unfitted body-horizontal comparator. Prior source responses and an earlier functional-model comparison were already inspected; exact LED frames and timing remain uncertain, so any result is conditional and not an independent biological holdout. No response-based selection or gain adjustment is permitted.

The first task is input-only resource admission, including exact sequence deduplication and full cell coverage; stop before any new voltage/response comparison if caps are exceeded. Fixed caps are 3,000 unique nonzero solves, 1,200 scientific child-CPU seconds, 512 MiB retained output and a 20 GiB free-space reserve. The pinned NEURON/NumPy environment was absent on November 14; the initial sandbox install failed package access and an authorized retry restored it under /tmp. No earlier model or evidence changed.

## 2026-11-14 — Combined T4-to-H2 comparison completed

The [comparison report](H2_T4_RECORDING_COMPARISON_REPORT_V1.md) records a real, previously inspected Zhao H2 recording, one location, eight directions and two polarities, using reconstructed approximate stimuli. The prespecified directional rule **fails**: hybrid error 14.313°, fixed body-horizontal reference 6.381°, and original functional baseline 1.393°; the hybrid is inside the 45° engineering guard but does not beat the reference. Adding the detailed left T4 population and assumed voltage-to-drive bridge worsened this directional metric on this recording. This does not isolate a faulty component or establish general biological invalidity; functional T5, approximate frames, male-model/female-recording differences and lack of measured voltage-to-release remain material limitations. [All conditions and decision](../results/h2_t4_recording_comparison_v1/decision.json).

Ten thousand repeat resamples of the same recording give descriptive 95% hybrid, reference and original error intervals of 4.38–23.50°, 0.35–15.57° and 0.16–11.10°, respectively; hybrid beats the reference in 2.13% of draws. The pre-prediction supplemental method does not alter the primary decision or supply across-animal uncertainty. [Repeat results](../results/h2_t4_recording_comparison_v1/repeat_uncertainty.json).

Numerical safeguards pass: 2,640 independently reviewed input samples, two cable refinements within 0.005 mV (maximum 0.001956 mV), fixed-case network refinement within 1e-8 (1.61e-11), independent endpoint/decision review and a two-graph replay (maximum 4.34e-18); thirteen focused tests pass. [Input review](../results/h2_t4_recording_comparison_v1/input_review.json), [cable review](../results/h2_t4_recording_comparison_v1/cable_numerics.json), [independent review](../results/h2_t4_recording_comparison_v1/independent_review.json). These establish numerical checks, not biological validation. The 1,162 cable solves used 466.80 CPU seconds and 72,781,824 bytes peak RSS, within frozen caps; graph stage used 81.51 CPU seconds and 248,590,336 bytes peak RSS. No bulk movies were regenerated. Snapshot and scoped post-exit verification follow.

## 2026-11-16 — Alignment assessment pilot specified and frozen

At the user's request, began a bounded test of the proposed methods-paper direction and an initial manuscript. The [protocol](ALIGNMENT_ASSESSMENT_PILOT_PROTOCOL_V1.md) freezes an archived-image audit across six already inspected T4 cells, quarter-pixel alignment sampling, and zero/0.5/1.0-LED-pixel Gaussian-blur sensitivities, plus a separate known-truth benchmark of three linear spatial filters with known observation noise. The benchmark must report correct-family rejection, wrong-family retention and unresolved outcomes rather than treating fewer rejections as a method advantage. [Before-analysis source and method lock](../results/alignment_assessment_pilot_v1/before_analysis.json). Six meaningful scalar-integration, boundary, and decision tests pass before analysis.

A material scope correction precedes outcomes: the original Gruntman experiment mapped receptive fields before the selected bar recordings and records nominal grid centres. The earlier project's exact aliases do not establish that the original experiment's full calibration was ambiguous. This new pilot will audit the available source metadata and report remaining mapping gaps. Already observed cells are development data; the blur scales are sensitivity assumptions, not measured optics. No new raw download, voltage simulation, neuronal-degeneracy claim, or biological validation is authorized by the computational results alone.

## 2026-11-16 — Alignment pilot completed with a limiting optical result

The [completed pilot report](ALIGNMENT_ASSESSMENT_PILOT_REPORT_V1.md) audits all 1,640 retained flash trials and 328 conditions for six previously inspected source T4 cells. Historical integer-shift inputs reproduce exactly. Across 289 quarter-pixel alignments per cell, the wider assumed Gaussian blur (SD 1 LED pixel) leaves only one input numerically equal to each cell's historical reference. This reaches the protocol's stopping condition for a blur-robust exact-ambiguity interpretation. It does not establish that the remaining differences are measurable in neural recordings or identify the true receptive-field position. The blur scales are assumed sensitivities, and extra blur could overlap with spatial filtering already absorbed in effective model inputs. [All image results](../results/alignment_assessment_pilot_v1/input_audit.json).

The original Gruntman paper explicitly performed two-dimensional RF mapping for all cells; that broader calibration is not the selected bar-data reanalysis. The present result cannot establish an omission or failed identification in the original experiment. All six cells are inspected development examples from one dataset, not new independent biological validation.

The separate frozen known-truth spatial-filter benchmark generated 45,000 datasets and 135,000 candidate-family assessments. At synthetic noise SD 0.02, the corrected single-fit baseline rejects the generating family in 8,989/15,000 cases (59.93%), versus 692/15,000 (4.61%) for a calibration-compatible set; however, 13,938/15,000 (92.92%) remain unresolved, and 6,793/30,000 (22.64%) other-family assessments are not rejected. At SD 0.10, 69.76% of other-family assessments pass at every retained alignment. This is an illustration of the rejection/decisiveness trade-off, not a demonstrated new calibration or superior mechanism-recovery method. [Summary](../results/alignment_assessment_pilot_v1/pilot_summary.json), [all corrected outcomes](../results/alignment_assessment_pilot_v1/numerical_ties_r2/synthetic_results.json).
